     1→import asyncio
     2→import json
     3→import logging
     4→from typing import AsyncIterator
     5→
     6→from claude_agent_sdk import AgentDefinition
     7→from app.database import Database
     8→from app.config import (
     9→    ANTHROPIC_MODEL, BIFROST_BASE_URL, BACKEND_BASE_URL,
    10→    LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY, LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY, LANGFUSE_BASE_URL, LANGFUSE_ENABLED,
    11→)
    12→from app.tracing import get_langfuse_client
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→
    17→def build_system_prompt(db: Database) -> str:
    18→    tables = db.list_tables()
    19→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    20→
    21→Task tool usage (CRITICAL — you MUST follow these rules):
    22→- When using the Task tool, you MUST set the "subagent_type" parameter to one of the named agents listed below. NEVER use generic values like "general-purpose", "code-writer", or any other value.
    23→- For any data question that requires SQL queries, set subagent_type to "sql-analyst"
    24→- For any visualization, chart, or graph request, set subagent_type to "chart-builder"
    25→- Do NOT set the "model" parameter on the Task tool — the named agents already have models configured. Omit the model field entirely.
    26→- After the chart-builder returns, do NOT repeat the chart JSON specification in your response. The chart is rendered automatically. Simply describe what the visualization shows in plain language.
    27→- Explain findings in plain language after getting results
    28→- Do NOT include SQL queries in your response unless the user explicitly asks to see the SQL. Focus on the results and insights, not the implementation details.
    29→
    30→Identity:
    31→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    32→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    33→
    34→Clarification:
    35→- When the user's request is ambiguous or could be interpreted in multiple ways, use the ask_user_question tool to ask for clarification before proceeding.
    36→- Provide 2-4 clear, concise options for the user to choose from.
    37→- Each option should have a short label and optional description.
    38→- Only ask when genuinely needed — don't over-ask for trivial decisions.
    39→"""
    40→    if not tables:
    41→        prompt += "\nNo tables are currently loaded. Ask the user to upload a CSV file first."
    42→    else:
    43→        prompt += "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    44→        for table in tables:
    45→            prompt += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    46→            for col in table["columns"]:
    47→                prompt += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    48→
    49→    return prompt
    50→
    51→
    52→def _build_table_schemas(db: Database) -> str:
    53→    """Build a text description of all loaded table schemas for subagent prompts."""
    54→    tables = db.list_tables()
    55→    if not tables:
    56→        return "\nNo tables are currently loaded."
    57→    text = "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
    58→    for table in tables:
    59→        text += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
    60→        for col in table["columns"]:
    61→            text += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
    62→    return text
    63→
    64→
    65→def build_subagent_definitions(db: Database) -> dict[str, AgentDefinition]:
    66→    """Build AgentDefinition objects for the sql-analyst and chart-builder subagents."""
    67→    table_schemas = _build_table_schemas(db)
    68→
    69→    sql_prompt = (
    70→        "You are a DuckDB SQL expert. Given a user's data question, write and execute "
    71→        "SQL queries to find the answer.\n\n"
    72→        "Guidelines:\n"
    73→        "- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries.\n"
    74→        "- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT).\n"
    75→        "- If a query fails, read the error message, fix the SQL, and retry.\n"
    76→        "- Use double quotes for table and column identifiers that might conflict "
    77→        "with reserved words.\n"
    78→        "- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results.\n"
    79→        + table_schemas
    80→    )
    81→
    82→    chart_prompt = (
    83→        "You are a data visualization expert using Plotly. Given a user's request for "
    84→        "a chart or visualization, query the data with SQL and then call the "
    85→        "`render_chart` tool with the Plotly spec.\n\n"
    86→        "Guidelines:\n"
    87→        "- Choose the most appropriate chart type (bar, line, scatter, pie, histogram, "
    88→        "box, heatmap, etc.).\n"
    89→        "- For pie charts, use `labels` and `values` fields in the trace.\n"
    90→        "- For multi-series data, group into separate traces.\n"
    91→        "- `layout.title` is required — always provide a descriptive title.\n"
    92→        "- Keep the chart clean and readable.\n\n"
    93→        "IMPORTANT — keep data small. Pre-aggregate in SQL instead of passing raw rows:\n"
    94→        "- Box plots: compute lowerfence, q1, median, q3, upperfence per group in SQL. "
    95→        "Use trace type \"box\" with those pre-computed fields instead of a raw `y` array.\n"
    96→        "- Histograms: compute bin counts with width_bucket() or CASE in SQL, then "
    97→        "render as a bar chart with the bin edges as `x` and counts as `y`.\n"
    98→        "- Scatter / line with many rows: sample (ORDER BY random() LIMIT 200) or "
    99→        "aggregate (e.g. average per time bucket) so each trace has at most ~200 points.\n"
   100→        "- General rule: each trace should have at most ~200 data points. "
   101→        "Large tool-call inputs cause malformed JSON and validation errors.\n\n"
   102→        "When ready to render, call `render_chart` with:\n"
   103→        "- `data`: array of Plotly trace objects\n"
   104→        "- `layout`: object containing at minimum {\"title\": \"<descriptive title>\"}\n\n"
   105→        "Do NOT output a JSON code block. Use the tool.\n"
   106→        + table_schemas
   107→    )
   108→
   109→    # The Claude Agent SDK only accepts 'sonnet' | 'opus' | 'haiku' | 'inherit' for
   110→    # the model field.  Arbitrary model strings (e.g. "openai/gpt-5.2-2025-12-11")
   111→    # cause the SDK to silently reject the agent definition, making it unavailable.
   112→    # Using "inherit" makes subagents use the orchestrator's ANTHROPIC_MODEL, which
   113→    # is routed through Bifrost to the correct provider.
   114→    return {
   115→        "sql-analyst": AgentDefinition(
   116→            description=(
   117→                "Use this agent for any data question that requires SQL queries "
   118→                "— exploring data, aggregations, filtering, joins, etc."
   119→            ),
   120→            prompt=sql_prompt,
   121→            tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"],
   122→            model="inherit",
   123→        ),
   124→        "chart-builder": AgentDefinition(
   125→            description=(
   126→                "Use this agent when the user wants a chart, graph, or visualization."
   127→            ),
   128→            prompt=chart_prompt,
   129→            tools=["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql", "mcp__duckdb__render_chart"],
   130→            model="inherit",
   131→        ),
   132→    }
   133→
   134→
   135→def _build_message_with_history(
   136→    message: str, conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None
   137→) -> str:
   138→    """Prepend conversation history context to the user message when editing."""
   139→    if not conversation_history:
   140→        return message
   141→
   142→    history_text = "Previous conversation (for context, I am now editing a message):\n"
   143→    for entry in conversation_history:
   144→        role = entry.get("role", "user").capitalize()
   145→        content = entry.get("content", "")
   146→        history_text += f"\n{role}: {content}\n"
   147→    history_text += f"\n---\n\nMy updated message:\n{message}"
   148→    return history_text
   149→
   150→
   151→
   152→def _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(
   153→    msg: dict, tool_names: dict[str, str], subagent_chart_specs: dict
   154→) -> None:
   155→    """Inspect an assistant message and capture render_chart input as chart_spec.
   156→
   157→    When the chart-builder subagent calls render_chart, the tool_use block
   158→    appears in an assistant message whose parent_tool_use_id is the Task
   159→    tool_use_id for the chart-builder.  We extract input directly so we never
   160→    need to parse free-text JSON.
   161→    """
   162→    parent_id = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   163→    if not parent_id or tool_names.get(parent_id) != "chart-builder":
   164→        return
   165→    for block in msg.get("message", {}).get("content", []):
   166→        if block.get("type") == "tool_use" and "render_chart" in block.get("name", ""):
   167→            subagent_chart_specs[parent_id] = block.get("input", {})
   168→            return
   169→
   170→
   171→async def stream_chat(
   172→    message: str,
   173→    session_id: str | None = None,
   174→    db: Database | None = None,
   175→    conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None,
   176→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None,
   177→    backend_session_id: str | None = None,
   178→) -> AsyncIterator[str]:
   179→    """Stream agent chat responses as SSE events via containerized sidecar."""
   180→    from app.container_manager import container_manager
   181→    if container_manager is None:
   182→        raise RuntimeError("Docker is not available. Container mode requires Docker.")
   183→    if db is None:
   184→        raise ValueError("db must be provided")
   185→
   186→    import httpx
   187→    import asyncio
   188→
   189→    query_message = _build_message_with_history(message, conversation_history)
   190→    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(db)
   191→
   192→    # Pass Langfuse credentials to the container so the sidecar's
   193→    # TypeScript Langfuse SDK can create traces directly.
   194→    env: dict[str, str] = {
   195→        "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": "placeholder",
   196→        "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   197→    }
   198→    if LANGFUSE_ENABLED:
   199→        env["LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY
   200→        env["LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY"] = LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY
   201→        env["LANGFUSE_BASE_URL"] = LANGFUSE_BASE_URL
   202→
   203→    # Use the backend session ID (X-Session-ID header) for both:
   204→    # 1. MCP SSE URL — so the container queries the correct DuckDB instance
   205→    # 2. Container lifecycle key — so the same container is reused across
   206→    #    requests from the same browser tab (the Claude agent session_id
   207→    #    changes after the first response, which would orphan the container)
   208→    stable_session = backend_session_id or session_id or "default"
   209→
   210→    try:
   211→        # Send SSE keepalive immediately so the HTTP response starts and
   212→        # intermediate proxies (Vite, nginx) don't drop the idle connection
   213→        # before we've finished the blocking Docker container creation.
   214→        yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   215→
   216→        # container_manager.create() is synchronous (blocking Docker API call).
   217→        # Run it in a thread executor so the event loop stays responsive and
   218→        # can continue flushing keepalives to the client during startup.
   219→        # gVisor (runsc) containers can take 10-30 seconds to spin up.
   220→        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   221→        create_future = loop.run_in_executor(None, container_manager.create, stable_session, env)
   222→
   223→        max_create_wait = 60.0
   224→        elapsed = 0.0
   225→        while not create_future.done():
   226→            await asyncio.sleep(2.0)
   227→            elapsed += 2.0
   228→            if elapsed >= max_create_wait:
   229→                create_future.cancel()
   230→                raise RuntimeError(f"Container creation timed out after {max_create_wait:.0f}s")
   231→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   232→        info = await create_future
   233→
   234→        # Wait for container to be ready
   235→        for attempt in range(10):
   236→            try:
   237→                async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(5.0)) as check_client:
   238→                    resp = await check_client.get(f"{info.url}/health")
   239→                    if resp.status_code == 200:
   240→                        break
   241→            except Exception:
   242→                pass
   243→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   244→            await asyncio.sleep(1)
   245→        else:
   246→            raise RuntimeError("Sidecar container failed health check after 10 attempts")
   247→
   248→        payload: dict = {
   249→            "message": query_message,
   250→            "session_id": session_id,
   251→            "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   252→            "model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL,
   253→            "mcp_server_url": f"{BACKEND_BASE_URL}/mcp/sse?session_id={stable_session}",
   254→            "env": {
   255→                "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY": "placeholder",
   256→                "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL": f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic",
   257→            },
   258→            "agents": {
   259→                name: {
   260→                    "description": agent_def.description,
   261→                    "prompt": agent_def.prompt,
   262→                    "tools": agent_def.tools,
   263→                    "model": agent_def.model,
   264→                }
   265→                for name, agent_def in build_subagent_definitions(db).items()
   266→            },
   267→        }
   268→        if langfuse_session_id:
   269→            payload["langfuse_session_id"] = langfuse_session_id
   270→        # Pass original message & history separately for Langfuse trace metadata
   271→        if conversation_history:
   272→            payload["original_message"] = message
   273→            payload["conversation_history"] = conversation_history
   274→
   275→        has_tool_calls = False
   276→        has_thinking = False
   277→        done_sent = False
   278→        waiting_for_user = False
   279→        tool_names: dict[str, str] = {}
   280→        tool_sqls: dict[str, str] = {}
   281→        # Track subagent text output from intermediate assistant messages.
   282→        # The TypeScript SDK's Task tool_result contains only metadata (agentId,
   283→        # usage), not the subagent's actual output.  The real output arrives in
   284→        # assistant messages whose parent_tool_use_id matches the Task tool ID.
   285→        subagent_texts: dict[str, str] = {}
   286→        subagent_chart_specs: dict[str, dict] = {}
   287→        actual_session_id = session_id
   288→
   289→        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0)) as client:
   290→            async with client.stream("POST", f"{info.url}/query", json=payload) as response:
   291→                line_iter = response.aiter_lines().__aiter__()
   292→                while True:
   293→                    try:
   294→                        if waiting_for_user:
   295→                            try:
   296→                                line = await asyncio.wait_for(line_iter.__anext__(), timeout=5.0)
   297→                            except asyncio.TimeoutError:
   298→                                yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   299→                                continue
   300→                        else:
   301→                            line = await line_iter.__anext__()
   302→                    except StopAsyncIteration:
   303→                        break
   304→
   305→                    if not line.startswith("data: "):
   306→                        continue
   307→                    raw = line[6:]
   308→                    waiting_for_user = False
   309→                    try:
   310→                        msg = json.loads(raw)
   311→                    except json.JSONDecodeError:
   312→                        continue
   313→
   314→                    msg_type = msg.get("type")
   315→                    # --- Token-level streaming events from SDK ---
   316→                    if msg_type == "stream_event":
   317→                        event = msg.get("event", {})
   318→                        event_type = event.get("type", "")
   319→                        stream_parent = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   320→                        is_subagent_event = bool(stream_parent and stream_parent in tool_names)
   321→
   322→                        if event_type == "content_block_delta":
   323→                            delta = event.get("delta", {})
   324→                            delta_type = delta.get("type", "")
   325→                            if delta_type == "thinking_delta":
   326→                                text = delta.get("thinking", "")
   327→                                if text and not is_subagent_event:
   328→                                    yield f"event: thinking\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   329→                            elif delta_type == "text_delta":
   330→                                text = delta.get("text", "")
   331→                                if text:
   332→                                    # Accumulate subagent text for chart_spec
   333→                                    # extraction (the SDK may not yield a
   334→                                    # complete assistant message for the
   335→                                    # subagent's final turn).
   336→                                    if is_subagent_event:
   337→                                        subagent_texts[stream_parent] = subagent_texts.get(stream_parent, "") + text
   338→                                    else:
   339→                                        event_name = "answer" if has_tool_calls else "thinking"
   340→                                        yield f"event: {event_name}\ndata: {json.dumps({'text': text})}\n\n"
   341→
   342→                        elif event_type == "content_block_start":
   343→                            block = event.get("content_block", {})
   344→                            block_type = block.get("type")
   345→                            if block_type == "thinking":
   346→                                if not is_subagent_event:
   347→                                    has_thinking = True
   348→                            elif block_type == "text":
   349→                                if has_thinking and not is_subagent_event:
   350→                                    yield f"event: thinking_done\ndata: {json.dumps({})}\n\n"
   351→                            elif block_type == "tool_use":
   352→                                if not is_subagent_event:
   353→                                    has_thinking = False
   354→                                    has_tool_calls = True
   355→
   356→                    # --- Complete assistant message (contains tool_use blocks) ---
   357→                    elif msg_type == "assistant":
   358→                        message_obj = msg.get("message", {})
   359→                        parent_tool_use_id = msg.get("parent_tool_use_id")
   360→
   361→                        # Capture text from subagent assistant messages.
   362→                        # These carry the real subagent output (e.g. chart JSON)
   363→                        # that the Task tool_result metadata lacks.
   364→                        if parent_tool_use_id and parent_tool_use_id in tool_names:
   365→                            text_parts = []
   366→                            for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   367→                                if block.get("type") == "text" and block.get("text"):
   368→                                    text_parts.append(block["text"])
   369→                            if text_parts:
   370→                                subagent_texts[parent_tool_use_id] = "\n".join(text_parts)
   371→                        _build_chart_spec_from_stream_messages(msg, tool_names, subagent_chart_specs)
   372→
   373→                        for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   374→                            block_type = block.get("type")
   375→                            if block_type == "tool_use":
   376→                                has_tool_calls = True
   377→                                tool_id = block.get("id", "")
   378→                                tool_name = block.get("name", "")
   379→                                tool_input = block.get("input", {})
   380→                                tool_names[tool_id] = tool_name
   381→                                is_execute_sql = "execute_sql" in tool_name
   382→                                sql = tool_input.get("sql", "") if is_execute_sql else ""
   383→                                if sql:
   384→                                    tool_sqls[tool_id] = sql
   385→                                tool_call_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": tool_name}
   386→                                if sql:
   387→                                    tool_call_data["sql"] = sql
   388→                                else:
   389→                                    tool_call_data["input"] = tool_input
   390→                                yield f"event: tool_call\ndata: {json.dumps(tool_call_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   391→                                if tool_name == "Task":
   392→                                    subagent_name = tool_input.get("subagent_type", "unknown")
   393→                                    subagent_prompt = tool_input.get("prompt", "")
   394→                                    tool_names[tool_id] = subagent_name
   395→                                    yield f"event: subagent_start\ndata: {json.dumps({'id': tool_id, 'name': subagent_name, 'prompt': subagent_prompt})}\n\n"
   396→
   397→                                # Detect ask_user_question tool
   398→                                if "ask_user_question" in tool_name:
   399→                                    from app.pending_questions import pending_question_store
   400→                                    import asyncio as _asyncio
   401→                                    for _ in range(50):
   402→                                        pending = pending_question_store.get_pending(stable_session)
   403→                                        if pending:
   404→                                            yield f"event: user_question\ndata: {json.dumps({'question_id': pending['question_id'], **pending['data']})}\n\n"
   405→                                            waiting_for_user = True
   406→                                            break
   407→                                        await _asyncio.sleep(0.1)
   408→
   409→                    # --- Tool results from user messages ---
   410→                    elif msg_type == "user":
   411→                        message_obj = msg.get("message", {})
   412→                        # The SDK attaches the subagent's actual output in
   413→                        # tool_use_result.content (a list of content blocks).
   414→                        # Extract it so we can use it for chart_spec extraction.
   415→                        tool_use_result = msg.get("tool_use_result")
   416→                        tool_use_result_text = ""
   417→                        if isinstance(tool_use_result, dict):
   418→                            tur_content = tool_use_result.get("content")
   419→                            if isinstance(tur_content, list):
   420→                                parts = []
   421→                                for item in tur_content:
   422→                                    if isinstance(item, dict) and item.get("type") == "text":
   423→                                        parts.append(item.get("text", ""))
   424→                                tool_use_result_text = "\n".join(parts)
   425→                        for block in message_obj.get("content", []):
   426→                            if block.get("type") != "tool_result":
   427→                                continue
   428→                            tool_id = block.get("tool_use_id", "")
   429→                            name = tool_names.get(tool_id, "")
   430→                            content_parts = block.get("content", [])
   431→                            text = ""
   432→                            if isinstance(content_parts, list):
   433→                                for part in content_parts:
   434→                                    if isinstance(part, dict) and part.get("type") == "text":
   435→                                        text = part.get("text", "")
   436→                            elif isinstance(content_parts, str):
   437→                                text = content_parts
   438→
   439→                            # Try to parse structured MCP result
   440→                            result_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": name}
   441→                            # Include the SQL from the original tool_call
   442→                            original_sql = tool_sqls.get(tool_id, "")
   443→                            if original_sql:
   444→                                result_data["sql"] = original_sql
   445→                            try:
   446→                                parsed = json.loads(text)
   447→                                if parsed.get("status") == "success":
   448→                                    if "chart_spec" in parsed:
   449→                                        result_data["chart_spec"] = parsed["chart_spec"]
   450→                                    else:
   451→                                        result_data["columns"] = parsed.get("columns", [])
   452→                                        result_data["rows"] = parsed.get("rows", [])[:100]
   453→                                        result_data["rowCount"] = parsed.get("rowCount", 0)
   454→                                elif parsed.get("status") == "error":
   455→                                    result_data["error"] = parsed.get("error", "")
   456→                                else:
   457→                                    result_data["output"] = text
   458→                            except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):
   459→                                result_data["output"] = text
   460→                            if block.get("is_error"):
   461→                                try:
   462→                                    parsed_err = json.loads(text)
   463→                                    result_data["error"] = parsed_err.get("error", text)
   464→                                except (json.JSONDecodeError, AttributeError):
   465→                                    result_data["error"] = text
   466→                            # Detect subagent result (Task tool)
   467→                            if name in ("sql-analyst", "chart-builder"):
   468→                                # Only chart-builder produces chart_spec JSON.
   469→                                # sql-analyst returns plain text results.
   470→                                if name == "chart-builder":
   471→                                    chart_spec = subagent_chart_specs.get(tool_id)
   472→                                    end_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": name}
   473→                                    if chart_spec:
   474→                                        end_data["chart_spec"] = chart_spec
   475→                                    else:
   476→                                        end_data["result"] = tool_use_result_text or subagent_texts.get(tool_id, text)
   477→                                        logger.warning(
   478→                                            "[container] render_chart tool_use not found for chart-builder %s",
   479→                                            tool_id,
   480→                                        )
   481→                                    yield f"event: subagent_end\ndata: {json.dumps(end_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   482→                                    continue
   483→                                else:
   484→                                    end_data: dict = {"id": tool_id, "name": name}
   485→                                    end_data["result"] = tool_use_result_text or subagent_texts.get(tool_id, text)
   486→                                    yield f"event: subagent_end\ndata: {json.dumps(end_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   487→                                    continue
   488→                            yield f"event: tool_result\ndata: {json.dumps(result_data, default=str)}\n\n"
   489→
   490→                    # --- Final result ---
   491→                    elif msg_type == "result":
   492→                        actual_session_id = msg.get("session_id") or actual_session_id
   493→                        if msg.get("is_error"):
   494→                            errors = msg.get("errors", [])
   495→                            error_text = msg.get("result") or "; ".join(errors) or "Unknown error"
   496→                            yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': error_text})}\n\n"
   497→                        yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   498→                        done_sent = True
   499→
   500→                    # --- Sidecar error (e.g. SDK/CLI crash inside container) ---
   501→                    elif msg_type == "error":
   502→                        error_text = msg.get("message") or "Sidecar error"
   503→                        logger.error("Sidecar reported error: %s", error_text)
   504→                        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': error_text})}\n\n"
   505→                        yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   506→                        done_sent = True
   507→
   508→                    # --- Extract session_id early from system init ---

   511→                        if sys_session:

   515→        if not done_sent:

   517→            yield f"event: done\ndata: {json.dumps({'session_id': actual_session_id})}\n\n"
   518→
   519→    except Exception as e:
   520→        logger.error("Container agent error: %s", str(e))
   521→        yield f"event: error\ndata: {json.dumps({'message': str(e)})}\n\n"
   522→    # Note: Container is kept alive for session resume (--resume flag) and is
   523→    # cleaned up by the background cleanup loop after
   524→    # CONTAINER_MAX_LIFETIME_SECONDS, or on application shutdown.

     1→from unittest.mock import MagicMock
     2→from app.database import Database
     3→from claude_agent_sdk import AgentDefinition
     4→
     5→
     6→def _make_db(tables=None):
     7→    db = MagicMock(spec=Database)
     8→    db.list_tables.return_value = tables or []
     9→    return db
    10→

    12→def test_build_subagent_definitions_returns_two_agents():
    13→    """Should return sql-analyst and chart-builder AgentDefinitions."""
    14→    from app.agent import build_subagent_definitions
    15→
    16→    db = _make_db()
    17→    agents = build_subagent_definitions(db)
    18→
    19→    assert "sql-analyst" in agents
    20→    assert "chart-builder" in agents
    21→    assert isinstance(agents["sql-analyst"], AgentDefinition)
    22→    assert isinstance(agents["chart-builder"], AgentDefinition)

    27→

    29→    agents = build_subagent_definitions(db)

    31→    assert agents["sql-analyst"].tools == ["mcp__duckdb__execute_sql"]

    58→def test_subagent_models_inherit():
    59→    from app.agent import build_subagent_definitions
    60→
    61→    db = _make_db()
    62→    agents = build_subagent_definitions(db)
    63→
    64→    # Subagents must use "inherit" so the SDK accepts them and they
    65→    # inherit the orchestrator's ANTHROPIC_MODEL at runtime.

  "content": "# Per-Subagent Model Routing Implementation Plan\n\n> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.\n\n**Goal:** Allow each subagent to use its own model when routing through a non-Anthropic proxy, using `REAL_MODEL@SDK_ALIAS` env var notation.\n\n**Architecture:** Parse `@suffix` from model env vars to extract SDK aliases (`haiku`/`sonnet`/`opus`) and real model names (`openai/gpt-4o`). Build a rewrite map at startup. app.config import parse_model\n    sdk, real = parse_model(\"\")\n    assert sdk == \"\"\n    assert real == \"\"\n\n\ndef test_build_model_rewrites_with_suffix():\n    from app.config import `backend/app/config.py`, replacing lines 8-13 (the current ANTHROPIC_MODEL and deprecation comment):\n\n```python\ndef parse_model(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Parse 'real_model@sdk_alias' -> (sdk_alias, real_model).\n\n    If no '@', returns (value, value) for backwards compatibility.\n    \"\"\"\n    if \"@\" in value:\n        real, sdk = value.rsplit(\"@\", 1)\n        return sdk, real\n    return value, value\n\n\ndef build_model_rewrites(pairs: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> dict[str, str]:\n    \"\"\"Build a rewrite map from (sdk_alias, real_model) pairs.\n\n    Only includes entries where sdk != real (i.e. rewriting is needed).\n    \"\"\"\n    return {sdk: real for sdk, real in pairs if sdk != real}\n\n\n_raw_model = os.getenv(\"ANTHROPIC_MODEL\", \"claude-sonnet-4-6\")\n_raw_sql = os.getenv(\"SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL\", \"inherit\")\n_raw_chart = os.getenv(\"CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL\", \"inherit\")\n\nANTHROPIC_MODEL_SDK, `backend/app/proxy.py`\n- Test: `backend/tests/test_proxy.py`\n\n**Step 1: Write the failing tests**\n\n```python\n# backend/tests/test_proxy.py\nimport json\nimport pytest\nfrom app.config import build_model_rewrites\nfrom app.proxy import rewrite_model_in_body\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_matching_tier():\n    rewrites = {\"sonnet\": \"openai/gpt-4o\"}\n    body = json.dumps({\"model\": \"claude-sonnet-4-6\", \"messages\": []}).encode()\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)\n    parsed = json.loads(result)\n    assert parsed[\"model\"] == \"openai/gpt-4o\"\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_haiku_full_name():\n    rewrites = {\"haiku\": \"openai/gpt-4o-mini\"}\n    body = json.dumps({\"model\": \"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001\", \"messages\": []}).encode()\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)\n    parsed = json.loads(result)\n    assert parsed[\"model\"] == \"openai/gpt-4o-mini\"\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_no_match():\n    rewrites = {\"sonnet\": \"openai/gpt-4o\"}\n    body = json.dumps({\"model\": \"claude-opus-4-6\", \"messages\": []}).encode()\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)\n    parsed = json.loads(result)\n    assert parsed[\"model\"] == \"claude-opus-4-6\"\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_empty_rewrites():\n    body = json.dumps({\"model\": \"claude-sonnet-4-6\", \"messages\": []}).encode()\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, {})\n    parsed = json.loads(result)\n    assert parsed[\"model\"] == \"claude-sonnet-4-6\"\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_no_model_field():\n    rewrites = {\"sonnet\": \"openai/gpt-4o\"}\n    body = json.dumps({\"messages\": []}).encode()\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)\n    parsed = json.loads(result)\n    assert \"model\" not in parsed\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_invalid_json_returns_unchanged():\n    rewrites = {\"sonnet\": \"openai/gpt-4o\"}\n    body = b\"not json\"\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)\n    assert result == body\n\n\ndef test_rewrite_model_short_alias():\n    \"\"\"SDK might send short alias directly (e.g. 'sonnet') without resolution.\"\"\"\n    rewrites = {\"sonnet\": \"openai/gpt-4o\"}\n    body = json.dumps({\"model\": \"sonnet\", \"messages\": []}).encode()\n    result = rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)\n    parsed = json.loads(result)\n    assert parsed[\"model\"] == \"openai/gpt-4o\"\n```\n\n**Step 2: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\nRun: `cd backend && python -m pytest tests/test_proxy.py -v`\nExpected: FAIL — `app.proxy` not found\n\n**Step 3: Create `backend/app/proxy.py`**\n\n```python\n# backend/app/proxy.py\nimport json\nimport logging\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Request\nfrom fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse\nimport httpx\n\nfrom app.config import MODEL_REWRITES, BIFROST_BASE_URL\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = BIFROST_BASE_URL + \"/anthropic\"\n\n_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {\n    \"host\", \"content-length\", \"transfer-encoding\", \"connection\",\n}\n_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {\"transfer-encoding\", \"content-encoding\", \"connection\"}\n\nrouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/anthropic\")\n\n\ndef rewrite_model_in_body(body: bytes, rewrites: dict[str, str]) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"Rewrite the 'model' field in a JSON body using the rewrites map.\n\n    Matches if the model string equals or contains a rewrite key\n    (e.g. 'sonnet' matches 'claude-sonnet-4-6').\n    Returns the body unchanged if no match or if body is not valid JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not rewrites:\n        return body\n    try:\n        data = json.loads(body)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):\n        return body\n    model = data.get(\"model\")\n    if not model or not isinstance(model, str):\n        return body\n    for tier, real_model in rewrites.items():\n        if model == tier or tier in model:\n            data[\"model\"] = real_model\n            return json.dumps(data).encode()\n    return body\n\n\n@router.api_route(\"/{path:path}\", methods=[\"GET\", \"POST\", \"PUT\", \"DELETE\", \"PATCH\"])\nasync def proxy_to_upstream(path: str, request: Request):\n    headers = {\n        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()\n        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS\n    }\n\n    body = await request.body()\n\n    # Rewrite model on POST requests (messages, completions, etc.)\n    if request.method == \"POST\" and MODEL_REWRITES:\n        body = rewrite_model_in_body(body, MODEL_REWRITES)\n\n    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))\n    try:\n        upstream_req = client.build_request(\n            method=request.method,\n            url=f\"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}\",\n            headers=headers,\n            content=body,\n        )\n        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)\n\n        response_headers = {\n            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()\n            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS\n        }\n\n        async def body_generator():\n            try:\n                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():\n                    yield chunk\n            finally:\n                await upstream_resp.aclose()\n                await client.aclose()\n\n        return StreamingResponse(\n            body_generator(),\n            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,\n            headers=response_headers,\n        )\n    except Exception:\n        await client.aclose()\n        logger.exception(\"Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s\", request.method, path)\n        raise\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd backend && python -m pytest tests/test_proxy.py -v`\nExpected: PASS (all 7 tests)\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/app/proxy.py backend/tests/test_proxy.py\ngit commit -m \"feat: add proxy with model rewriting for @suffix routing\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 3: Wire proxy into main.py and route SDK through it\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `backend/app/main.py:10-11` (add proxy import)\n- Modify: `backend/app/main.py:60-65` (add router)\n\n**Step 1: Add proxy router to main.py**\n\nAdd import at line 11 (after existing route imports):\n```python\nfrom app.proxy import router as proxy_router\n```\n\nAdd router registration after line 65 (after `session.router`):\n```python\napp.include_router(proxy_router)\n```\n\n**Step 2: Verify the app still starts and proxy route is ---

## Report

### What I implemented

Added two helper functions and model config parsing to `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/config.py`:

1. **`parse_model(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]`** -- Parses the `real_model@sdk_alias` format. If no `@` is present, returns `(value, value)` for backwards compatibility.

2. **`build_model_rewrites(pairs: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> dict[str, str]`** -- Builds a rewrite map from `(sdk_alias, real_model)` pairs, only including entries where the SDK alias differs from the real model name.

3. **Module-level config variables** -- Parses three env vars (`ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL`) through `parse_model()`, producing `*_SDK` and `*_REAL` pairs plus a `MODEL_REWRITES` dict. The `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` backwards-compat alias is preserved.

### What I tested and test results

Created `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_model_parsing.py` with 7 tests:

| Test | Verifies |
|------|----------|
| `test_parse_model_with_at_suffix` | `"openai/gpt-4o@sonnet"` splits correctly |
| `test_parse_model_without_suffix` | `"haiku"` returns identity pair |
| `test_parse_model_full_anthropic_name` | `"claude-sonnet-4-6"` returns identity pair |
| `test_parse_model_empty_string` | Empty string handled gracefully |
| `test_build_model_rewrites_with_suffix` | Multiple rewrite pairs produce correct dict |
| `test_build_model_rewrites_skips_same` | Identity pairs (sdk == real) are excluded |
| `test_build_model_rewrites_empty` | All-identity input produces empty dict |

**Results:** All 7 new tests pass. The existing `test_subagent_config::test_anthropic_model_has_default` also passes, confirming backwards compatibility. Pre-existing failures in `test_session_manager` and `test_container_manager` are unrelated (sandbox file permissions and missing `docker` module).

---

## Code Review: Task 1 -- `parse_model()` helper and `@suffix` env var parsing

**Commits reviewed:** `40c32611..4af91726` (1 commit: `feat: add parse_model() helper and @suffix env var parsing`)

**Files changed:**
- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/config.py` (modified)
- `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_model_parsing.py` (new)

---

### Strengths

1. **Exact plan alignment.** The implementation matches the plan in `docs/plans/2026-02-27-per-subagent-model-routing.md` Task 1 essentially character-for-character. The functions, variable names, test cases, and even the commit message match the plan specification. No deviations from the plan were found.

2. **Backward compatibility preserved.** The `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` alias on line 36 of `config.py` ensures that all existing consumers (`agent.py` line 9 and 252, `test_subagent_config.py` line 1) continue to work without modification. When no `@` suffix is used, `parse_model()` returns `(value, value)`, so `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` resolves to the same string as before.

3. **Clean, focused functions.** Both `parse_model()` and `build_model_rewrites()` are pure functions with no side effects, clear docstrings, and single responsibilities. They are easy to test in isolation.

4. **Use of `rsplit` is correct.** By using `rsplit("@", 1)`, the parser correctly handles model strings that might contain `@` in the real model portion (unlikely but defensively correct). Only the last `@` is treated as the delimiter.

5. **All 7 new tests pass.** Verified by running `python -m pytest tests/test_model_parsing.py -v`. No regressions in the tests that can run locally (the 3 collection errors are pre-existing, caused by missing `docker` module -- unrelated to this change).

6. **Good test coverage for the two functions.** Tests cover: `@` suffix present, no suffix, full Anthropic model name, empty string, rewrites with differences, rewrites with same values, and an all-same rewrite list.

---

### Issues

#### Important (should fix)

1. **Missing blank line between `BACKEND_BASE_URL` and `def parse_model`.**

   In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/config.py`, lines 7-8:

   ```python
   BACKEND_BASE_URL = os.getenv("BACKEND_BASE_URL", "http://duckdb-data-agent:10000")
   def parse_model(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
   ```

   PEP 8 requires two blank lines before a top-level function definition. The previous code had the `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` assignment and comment block here, and when they were replaced, the blank line separator was lost. This should be:

   ```python
   BACKEND_BASE_URL = os.getenv("BACKEND_BASE_URL", "http://duckdb-data-agent:10000")


   def parse_model(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
   ```

   Note: The plan document itself also omits this blank line (it says "replacing lines 8-13"), so the plan has a minor formatting gap. This is a simple fix.

2. **Unused import in test file.**

   In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_model_parsing.py`, lines 1-2:

   ```python
   import os
   from unittest.mock import patch
   ```

   Neither `os` nor `patch` is used anywhere in the test file. These were likely carried over from the plan document which included them anticipating future tests (or integration tests that mock env vars). They should be removed to keep the test file clean. This matches the plan verbatim, so it is a plan-inherited issue.

#### Minor (nice to have)

3. **No test for multiple `@` characters in the value.**

   While `rsplit("@", 1)` handles this correctly by design, an explicit test like `parse_model("provider/model@v2@sonnet")` returning `("sonnet", "provider/model@v2")` would document this edge case behavior. This is a low-priority suggestion since the current behavior is correct.

4. **No validation of SDK alias values.**

   `parse_model()` accepts any string as the SDK alias. If a user writes `REDACTED@invalid-alias`, the system would silently produce an SDK alias of `"invalid-alias"` which the Claude Agent SDK would reject at runtime. An optional validation step (checking against `{"haiku", "sonnet", "opus", "inherit"}` or known full model names) could catch misconfiguration early. However, this might be intentionally deferred -- the plan does not call for it, and it could be added as a separate enhancement.

5. **`_raw_*` variables exposed at module level.**

   The `_raw_model`, `_raw_sql`, and `_raw_chart` variables on lines 27-29 use underscore-prefix convention to indicate "private," which is appropriate. No issue here, just noting the convention is correctly applied.

---

### Assessment: **Approved**

The implementation is a faithful, clean execution of Task 1 from the plan. The two functions are well-designed pure functions, backward compatibility is properly maintained, and test coverage is solid. The two "Important" items (missing blank line and unused imports) are trivial fixes that do not affect correctness or behavior. The code is ready to proceed to Task 2 (creating the proxy with model rewriting).

**Summary of action items:**
- Add two blank lines before `def parse_model()` in `config.py` (PEP 8 compliance)
- Remove unused `import os` and `from unittest.mock import patch` from `test_model_parsing.py`

\"openai/gpt-4o\"\n```\n\n**Step 2: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\nRun: `cd backend && python -m pytest tests/test_proxy.py -v`\nExpected: FAIL — `app.proxy` not found\n\n**Step 3: Create `backend/app/proxy.py`**\n\n```python\nimport json\nimport logging\n\nfrom fastapi import APIRouter, Request\nfrom fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse\nimport httpx\n\nfrom app.config import MODEL_REWRITES, BIFROST_BASE_URL\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\nANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = BIFROST_BASE_URL + \"/anthropic\"\n\n_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {\n    \"host\", \"content-length\", \"transfer-encoding\", \"connection\",\n}\n_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {\"transfer-encoding\", \"content-encoding\", \"connection\"}\n\nrouter = APIRouter(prefix=\"/anthropic\")\n\n\ndef rewrite_model_in_body(body: bytes, rewrites: dict[str, str]) -> bytes:\n    \"\"\"Rewrite the 'model' field in a JSON body using the rewrites map.\n\n    Matches if the model string equals or contains a rewrite key\n    (e.g. 'sonnet' matches 'claude-sonnet-4-6').\n    Returns the body unchanged if no match or if body is not valid JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not rewrites:\n        return body\n    try:\n        data = json.loads(body)\n    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):\n        return body\n    model = data.get(\"model\")\n    if not model or not isinstance(model, str):\n        return body\n    for tier, real_model in rewrites.items():\n        if model == tier or tier in model:\n            data[\"model\"] = real_model\n            return json.dumps(data).encode()\n    return body\n\n\n@router.api_route(\"/{path:path}\", methods=[\"GET\", \"POST\", \"PUT\", \"DELETE\", \"PATCH\"])\nasync def proxy_to_upstream(path: str, request: Request):\n    headers = {\n        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()\n        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS\n    }\n\n    body = await request.body()\n\n    # Rewrite model on POST requests (messages, completions, etc.)\n    if request.method == \"POST\" and MODEL_REWRITES:\n        body = rewrite_model_in_body(body, MODEL_REWRITES)\n\n    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))\n    try:\n        upstream_req = client.build_request(\n            method=request.method,\n            url=f\"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}\",\n            headers=headers,\n            content=body,\n        )\n        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)\n\n        response_headers = {\n            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()\n            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS\n        }\n\n        async def body_generator():\n            try:\n                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():\n                    yield chunk\n            finally:\n                await upstream_resp.aclose()\n                await client.aclose()\n\n        return StreamingResponse(\n            body_generator(),\n            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,\n            headers=response_headers,\n        )\n    except Exception:\n        await client.aclose()\n        logger.exception(\"Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s\", request.method, path)\n        raise\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests to verify they pass**\n\nRun: `cd backend && python -m pytest tests/test_proxy.py -v`\nExpected: PASS (all 7 tests)\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add backend/app/proxy.py backend/tests/test_proxy.py\ngit commit -m \"feat: add proxy with model rewriting for @suffix routing\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.6 <noreply@anthropic.com>\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nThis is Task 2 of 6. Task 1 (config parsing with `parse_model()`, `build_model_rewrites()`, and `MODEL_REWRITES`) is already complete. This task creates the proxy that uses `MODEL_REWRITES` to intercept and rewrite model names in API requests before forwarding to the upstream (Bifrost).\n\nThe proxy will be wired into `main.py` in Task 3. ## Report

### What I implemented

**`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/proxy.py`** -- A FastAPI reverse proxy module with two main components:

1. **`rewrite_model_in_body(body, rewrites)`** -- A pure function that parses a JSON request body, checks if the `model` field matches any key in the rewrites dictionary (either exact match or substring match), and replaces it with the target model. Handles edge cases: invalid JSON, missing model field, empty rewrites map.

2. **`proxy_to_upstream` route** -- A catch-all route under `/anthropic/{path:path}` that forwards requests to `BIFROST_BASE_URL/anthropic/{path}`. On POST requests, it applies model rewriting using `MODEL_REWRITES` from config. Uses streaming responses via `httpx.AsyncClient` with a 300-second timeout. Properly filters hop-by-hop headers in both directions.

### What I tested and test results

**`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`** -- 7 unit tests for `rewrite_model_in_body`:

| Test | Description | Result |
|------|-------------|--------|
| `test_rewrite_model_matching_tier` | `sonnet` matches `claude-sonnet-4-6` | PASS |
| `test_rewrite_model_haiku_full_name` | `haiku` matches `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` | PASS |
| `test_rewrite_model_no_match` | `sonnet` does not match `claude-opus-4-6` | PASS |
| `test_rewrite_model_empty_rewrites` | Empty rewrites returns body unchanged | PASS |
| `test_rewrite_model_no_model_field` | Missing model field returns body unchanged | PASS |
| `test_rewrite_model_invalid_json_returns_unchanged` | Non-JSON body returned as-is | PASS |
| `test_rewrite_model_short_alias` | Direct alias `sonnet` matches itself | PASS |

All 7 tests pass. Existing `test_model_parsing.py` tests also pass (7/7) -- no regressions.

### Files changed

- **Created:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/proxy.py` (90 lines)
- **Created:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_proxy.py` (48 lines)

### Self-review findings

The implementation is clean and matches the task specification exactly. A few observations:

1. **Substring matching order**: The `for tier, real_model in rewrites.items()` loop matches the first key found. In Python 3.7+ dicts preserve insertion order, and the rewrites are built deterministically from config, so this is predictable. If two keys could both substring-match the same model (e.g., `"son"` and `"sonnet"`), whichever was inserted first wins. In practice, the keys are tier names like `"sonnet"`, `"haiku"`, `"opus"` which are distinct enough that this is not a concern.

2. **No tests for the async proxy route**: The tests focus on the pure `rewrite_model_in_body` function. Testing the full proxy endpoint would require mocking `httpx.AsyncClient` and is better suited for integration testing (Task 6). This is acceptable for this task.

3. **httpx client created per-request**: A new `httpx.AsyncClient` is created per request rather than using a shared client. This is simpler but less efficient under high concurrency. For a proxy that primarily serves a single Claude SDK client, this is fine.

### Issues or concerns

None. The module is ready for Task 3 (wiring into `main.py`).

## Code Review: Task 2 -- Create Proxy with Model Rewriting

**Commit:** `a0bb2f82` -- "feat: add proxy with model rewriting for @suffix routing"
**Files changed:** `backend/app/proxy.py` (new, 89 lines), `backend/tests/test_proxy.py` (new, 59 lines)

---

### Plan Alignment

The implementation matches the plan from Task 2 in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/docs/plans/2026-02-27-per-subagent-model-routing.md` essentially line-for-line. The code in `proxy.py` is identical to the plan's Step 3 specification, the tests in `test_proxy.py` are identical to the plan's Step 1 specification, and the commit message matches the plan's Step 5. There are zero deviations from the plan. This is a faithful, precise implementation.

---

### Strengths

1. **Clean separation of concerns.** The `rewrite_model_in_body` function is a pure function that takes bytes and a rewrite dict, making it highly testable in isolation from the HTTP proxy machinery.

2. **Defensive input handling.** The rewrite function handles invalid JSON gracefully (returns body unchanged), handles missing `model` field, handles non-string model values, and short-circuits on empty rewrite maps. This is solid defensive programming.

3. **Proper streaming proxy pattern.** The `body_generator` uses a `try/finally` to ensure both the upstream response and the httpx client are closed, even if the downstream consumer disconnects mid-stream. This prevents resource leaks.

4. **Good header filtering.** The `_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS` and `_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS` sets correctly strip hop-by-hop headers that should not be forwarded through a proxy.

5. **Comprehensive test coverage for `rewrite_model_in_body`.** All seven tests cover distinct scenarios: exact match, substring match (full model name), no match, empty rewrites, missing model field, invalid JSON, and short alias. This is thorough.

6. **Consistent codebase patterns.** The file uses `APIRouter` with a prefix, `logging.getLogger(__name__)`, and follows the same import style as other modules in `backend/app/`.

---

### Issues

#### Important (Should Fix)

**1. Substring matching in `rewrite_model_in_body` is ambiguous and order-dependent.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/proxy.py`, lines 38-41:

```python
for tier, real_model in rewrites.items():
    if model == tier or tier in model:
        data["model"] = real_model
        return json.dumps(data).encode()
```

The `tier in model` check does a substring match, which means:
- If `rewrites = {"son": "x", "sonnet": "y"}`, the key `"son"` would match `"claude-sonnet-4-6"` before `"sonnet"` gets a chance, depending on dict iteration order.
- More realistically, if someone configures both `"haiku"` and `"opus"` as rewrite keys, a model name hypothetically containing both substrings would match whichever appears first.
- A tier key like `"o"` would match nearly everything.

While the current use case (short tier names like `"sonnet"`, `"haiku"`, `"opus"`) makes collisions unlikely, the function's behavior depends on dict iteration order, which is fragile. Consider either (a) matching longest key first by sorting `rewrites.items()` by key length descending, or (b) using a more targeted matching strategy such as checking if the tier appears as a word boundary within the model string.

**2. New `httpx.AsyncClient` created on every request.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/proxy.py`, line 58:

```python
client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
```

A new `AsyncClient` is instantiated per request. This means a new connection pool, new TLS handshake, and new TCP connection for every proxied request. For a proxy that will handle every SDK API call (potentially many per chat session), this is inefficient. The `httpx` documentation recommends reusing clients. Consider creating a module-level client or using FastAPI's lifespan to manage a shared client instance. Example:

```python
_client: httpx.AsyncClient | None = None

def get_client() -> httpx.AsyncClient:
    global _client
    if _client is None:
        _client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
    return _client
```

Or better yet, initialize it in a FastAPI lifespan handler and close it on shutdown.

**3. No tests for the proxy endpoint itself (only the rewrite function).**

The test file at `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_proxy.py` only tests `rewrite_model_in_body`. There are no integration tests for the `proxy_to_upstream` endpoint. The existing codebase pattern (see `test_respond_endpoint.py`) uses `TestClient(app)` for endpoint-level tests. While the proxy endpoint requires the router to be wired in (Task 3) and a mock upstream, the plan explicitly scoped this task's tests to the rewrite function only, so this is more of a forward-looking observation -- integration tests should be considered in a later task.

#### Minor (Nice to Have)

**4. Unused `pytest` import in test file.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, line 2:

```python
import pytest
```

This import is never used in the file. No test uses `pytest.raises`, `pytest.fixture`, or any other pytest API. It should be removed to keep the code clean.

**5. Unused `build_model_rewrites` import in test file.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_proxy.py`, line 3:

```python
from app.config import build_model_rewrites
```

This import is never used in any test. All tests construct their rewrite dicts inline. It should be removed.

**6. `content-length` stripped from request headers but not recalculated after body rewrite.**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/proxy.py`, the `content-length` header is stripped in `_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS` (line 15), and the rewritten body is passed as `content=body` to `client.build_request` (line 64). The `httpx` library will set `Content-Length` correctly when `content` is provided as bytes, so this works. However, the code would benefit from a brief comment noting this is intentional (relying on httpx to set the correct `Content-Length` for the potentially rewritten body).

**7. The 300-second timeout is undocumented.**

The `httpx.Timeout(300.0)` on line 58 is a 5-minute timeout. For long-running LLM streaming responses, this may or may not be sufficient. Consider making this configurable or at least adding a comment explaining the rationale.

---

### Security Considerations

- **No API key leakage risk.** The proxy forwards headers from the incoming request (which include the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set by the SDK). Since this proxy runs inside the backend container and forwards to the Bifrost sidecar on the same Docker network, there is no external exposure.
- **Path traversal in URL construction.** The URL is constructed as `f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}"` where `path` comes from the request. Since `ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM` is a fixed value and `httpx` handles URL encoding, there is no significant risk here, but it is worth noting that the proxy will forward any path segment to upstream.
- **No rate limiting or request size limits.** The proxy reads the full body into memory with `await request.body()`. For normal API traffic this is fine, but there is no guard against extremely large payloads. This is acceptable given the internal-only nature of the proxy.

---

### Assessment: **Approved with minor comments**

The implementation is clean, well-tested for its stated scope, and faithfully follows the plan. The code is production-ready for its purpose as an internal model-rewriting proxy.

The two **Important** items (substring match ambiguity and per-request client instantiation) are not blockers for merging this task but should be addressed before the feature ships to production:

- The substring matching issue (Item 1) could cause incorrect rewrites if the rewrite map grows or if users configure unexpected tier names. A targeted fix would add negligible complexity.
- The per-request client issue (Item 2) will cause unnecessary overhead under load. This is a straightforward optimization that could be done in Task 3 when the proxy is wired into `main.py` (the lifespan handler is the natural place to manage the shared client).

The **Minor** items (unused imports, missing comments) are trivial cleanups that can be done in a follow-up pass.

**Step 6: Self-review and report.**

Here is a summary of all changes made for Task 4:

## Files Modified

### 1. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/app/agent.py`

**Subagent definitions (lines 112-130):** Removed the outdated comment about SDK model constraints. Changed `model="inherit"` to `model=SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK` for the sql-analyst subagent and `model=CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK` for the chart-builder subagent. These config values come from `parse_model()` in Task 1, allowing per-subagent model routing via `@suffix` notation in env vars.

**Env dict for container (line 194):** Changed `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from `f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic"` to `f"{BACKEND_BASE_URL}/anthropic"`. This routes SDK API calls through the backend proxy (which does model rewriting) instead of directly to Bifrost.

**Payload model (line 250):** Changed `"model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL` to `"model": ANTHROPIC_MODEL_SDK`, using the SDK alias that the Claude Agent SDK accepts.

**Payload env (line 254):** Changed `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from `f"{BIFROST_BASE_URL}/anthropic"` to `f"{BACKEND_BASE_URL}/anthropic"`, same routing change as the env dict above.

### 2. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_subagent_definitions.py`

Replaced `test_subagent_models_inherit()` with `test_subagent_models_use_sdk_aliases()`. The new test imports `SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK` and `CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK` from config and asserts that subagent definitions use those values instead of hardcoded `"inherit"`.

### 3. `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/litellm-proxy/backend/tests/test_subagent_config.py`

> AGENT

All 6 tasks complete. Here's the summary:

**5 commits implementing per-subagent model routing:**

1. **`3a29de9c`** - `parse_model()` helper + `build_model_rewrites()` in config.py, 7 tests
2. **`88a943c3`** - `proxy.py` with `rewrite_model_in_body()` and streaming proxy route, 7 tests
3. **`e040d01f`** - Wire proxy router into main.py
4. **`a5ea6db6`** - agent.py uses SDK aliases for subagents, routes through backend proxy
5. **`e7f8337a`** - .env.example documentation

**How to use it:** Add `@suffix` to your model env vars:

```env
REDACTED@sonnet
REDACTED@haiku
REDACTED@haiku
```

Without `@suffix`, everything works exactly as before (backwards compatible).

     9→
    10→def parse_model(value: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
    11→    """Parse 'real_model@sdk_alias' -> (sdk_alias, real_model).
    12→
    13→    If no '@', returns (value, value) for backwards compatibility.
    14→    """
    15→    if "@" in value:
    16→        real, sdk = value.rsplit("@", 1)
    17→        return sdk, real
    18→    return value, value
    19→
    20→
    21→def build_model_rewrites(pairs: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> dict[str, str]:
    22→    """Build a rewrite map from (sdk_alias, real_model) pairs.
    23→
    24→    Only includes entries where sdk != real (i.e. rewriting is needed).
    25→    """
    26→    return {sdk: real for sdk, real in pairs if sdk != real}
    27→
    28→
    29→_raw_model = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-4-6")
    30→_raw_sql = os.getenv("SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL", "inherit")
    31→_raw_chart = os.getenv("CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL", "inherit")
    32→
    33→ANTHROPIC_MODEL_SDK, ANTHROPIC_MODEL_REAL = parse_model(_raw_model)
    34→SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL = parse_model(_raw_sql)
    35→CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL = parse_model(_raw_chart)
    36→
    37→# Backwards compat alias — existing code that reads ANTHROPIC_MODEL gets the SDK alias
    38→ANTHROPIC_MODEL = ANTHROPIC_MODEL_SDK
    39→
    40→MODEL_REWRITES = build_model_rewrites([
    41→    (ANTHROPIC_MODEL_SDK, ANTHROPIC_MODEL_REAL),
    42→    (SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL),
    43→    (CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK, CHART_SUBAGENT_MODEL_REAL),
    44→])
    45→
    46→LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_PUBLIC_KEY", "")
    47→LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_SECRET_KEY", "")
    48→LANGFUSE_BASE_URL = os.getenv("LANGFUSE_BASE_URL", "https://cloud.langfuse.com")

> AGENT

The error is `nodename nor servname provided, or not known` — the proxy is trying to connect to `bifrost:8080` (the Docker hostname) which doesn't exist when running locally.

The proxy upstream is hardcoded to `BIFROST_BASE_URL + "/anthropic"`, but when running locally (not in Docker), `bifrost:8080` is unresolvable.

Let me check and fix.

> AGENT

The proxy forwards to `BIFROST_BASE_URL/anthropic` (defaults to `http://bifrost:8080/anthropic`). But in your local setup, you don't have a Bifrost service — API calls should go directly to the Anthropic API (or a LiteLLM proxy).

The fix: make the upstream URL configurable via an env var, defaulting to `https://api.anthropic.com`.

     1→import json
     2→import logging
     3→
     4→from fastapi import APIRouter, Request
     5→from fastapi.responses import StreamingResponse
     6→import httpx
     7→
     8→from app.config import MODEL_REWRITES, BIFROST_BASE_URL
     9→
    10→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    11→
    12→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = BIFROST_BASE_URL + "/anthropic"
    13→
    14→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    15→    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    16→}
    17→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    18→
    19→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
    20→
    21→
    22→def rewrite_model_in_body(body: bytes, rewrites: dict[str, str]) -> bytes:
    23→    """Rewrite the 'model' field in a JSON body using the rewrites map.
    24→
    25→    Matches if the model string equals or contains a rewrite key
    26→    (e.g. 'sonnet' matches 'claude-sonnet-4-6').
    27→    Returns the body unchanged if no match or if body is not valid JSON.
    28→    """
    29→    if not rewrites:
    30→        return body
    31→    try:
    32→        data = json.loads(body)
    33→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    34→        return body
    35→    model = data.get("model")
    36→    if not model or not isinstance(model, str):
    37→        return body
    38→    for tier, real_model in rewrites.items():
    39→        if model == tier or tier in model:
    40→            data["model"] = real_model
    41→            return json.dumps(data).encode()
    42→    return body
    43→
    44→
    45→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
    46→async def proxy_to_upstream(path: str, request: Request):
    47→    headers = {
    48→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
    49→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
    50→    }
    51→
    52→    body = await request.body()
    53→
    54→    # Rewrite model on POST requests (messages, completions, etc.)
    55→    if request.method == "POST" and MODEL_REWRITES:
    56→        body = rewrite_model_in_body(body, MODEL_REWRITES)
    57→
    58→    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
    59→    try:
    60→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
    61→            method=request.method,
    62→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
    63→            headers=headers,
    64→            content=body,
    65→        )
    66→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
    67→
    68→        response_headers = {
    69→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
    70→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
    71→        }
    72→
    73→        async def body_generator():
    74→            try:
    75→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
    76→                    yield chunk
    77→            finally:
    78→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
    79→                await client.aclose()
    80→
    81→        return StreamingResponse(
    82→            body_generator(),
    83→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
    84→            headers=response_headers,
    85→        )
    86→    except Exception:
    87→        await client.aclose()
    88→        logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
    89→        raise
    90→

> AGENT

The SDK gets `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=placeholder`. The proxy needs to replace it with the real key before forwarding to Anthropic. But there's also an `OPENAI_API_KEY` needed when rewriting to OpenAI models. Let me think about this...

When the model is rewritten to `openai/gpt-5-mini-2025-08-07`, the upstream needs to be a proxy that understands the `openai/` prefix (like LiteLLM). If forwarding directly to `api.anthropic.com`, Anthropic won't understand `openai/gpt-5-mini`.

> AGENT

OK, so for mixed providers the proxy needs to route based on the model. But there's a fundamental challenge: the Claude Agent SDK speaks **Anthropic API format** (`/v1/messages`). Sending an Anthropic-format request to OpenAI's API directly won't work — the request/response formats are different.

For non-Anthropic models to work, you need a **translation proxy** like LiteLLM that accepts Anthropic API format and translates to/from OpenAI format.

Let me fix the immediate error first (proxy can't resolve `bifrost:8080`), and add proper API key injection so Anthropic models work. Then I'll explain the path forward for OpenAI models.
